SUBCONTRACTOR, provided that such right of the CONTRACTOR shall be in addition to its other rights and
remediesunderthisCONTRACTandLAW.

by reason of a FORCE MAJEURE EVENT, the CONTRACTOR shall have the right at any time, at its sole

1. GeneralProvisions
1.1 SUBCONTRACTORHSEPlanApproval
SUBCONTRACTOR shall provide a written Health Safety and Environment Plan (HSE Plan) to
WISON in accordance with the following outlines. This shall be completed and shall be approved in
writingbyWISONpriortotheSUBCONTRACTORmobilizingtothesite.
The SUBCONTRACTOR HSE Plan shall be compiled based on WISON HSE Plan and relevant
HSEprocedures,OWNERHSErequirementsandlocalLawsandRegulations.
This management system shall be amended when operations or conditions require and such
amendmentsshallbesubmittedtotheWISONforreview andacceptance.
SUBCONTRACTOR, its agents and employees shall comply with the HSE Plan and all applicable
health, safety and environmental Laws and Regulations. SUBCONTRACTOR shall take or cause to
be taken any additional measures under the direction of WISON HSE Manager to prevent the injury
or death of any person, or any damage or loss of property during SUBCONTRACTOR performance
ofthePROJECT.
WISON will monitor and inspect any PROJECT Site for compliance with the above referenced
Health,SafetyandEnvironmentalPlanandRequirements.
1.2 SUBCONTRACTORPROJECTHSEPlan
If not accomplished during the bid phase, SUBCONTRACTOR shall prepare and submit a written
HSE Plan to WISON HSE Manager for approval on the Effective Date. The performance of any
portionofthePROJECT shallonlyproceedin accordancewith theHSEplanagreedbyWISON and
SUBCONTRACTOR. If not agreed prior to the Effective Date the HSE plan shall be submitted and
agreedpriortotheexecutionofanywork.
SUBCONTRACTOR HSE Plan shall provide as a minimum meet PR-5312-1000-SA-0003
ConstructionHSEPlanandPR-5312-1000-SA-0001ProjectHSEPlan Requirements.
1.2.1 HSEManager/Representatives
SUBCONTRACTOR shall employ at least one HSE professional (Management Level with extensive
experience working with international contractors) and additional HSE Supervisor(s) acceptable to
WISON, submitting resumes and credentials for WISON review, verification, and acceptance. HSE
Supervisor(s)shallberesidentonthePROJECTforallSUBCONTRACTORworkactivities.
SUBCONTRACTOR HSE Supervisor(s) and their staff shall have sufficient authority and control to
ensure effectiveness of the HSE process and SUBCONTRACTOR shall hold them accountable for
facilitating its implementation. SUBCONTRACTOR shall maintain a minimum ratio of HSE
Supervisors to employees of 1:30 on site. Additional HSE Supervisors shall be mobilized as
required by the CONTRACTOR, particularly for high-risk activities, night shift operations, live plant
work, or any other conditions deemed to require enhanced HSE oversight to ensure full compliance
withProjectandADNOCHSErequirements.
The quantity and qualification requirements for the HSE Manager, HSE Engineer, HSE Officer,
Hygiene Officer, Road Safety Coordinator, Environmental Engineer, and other HSE personnel shall
comply with APPENDIX 2 – ADNOC HSEWM Minimum Requirements in Contracts, with reference
toClause6.18–HSEOrganization.
The number of vehicles allocated for the Road Safety Coordinator and the Transport Safety
Management Team (TSMT) shall also comply with the requirements stipulated in APPENDIX 2 –

6. IncidentReporting
SUBCONTRACTOR shall ensure that an immediate oral report is made to the WISON
Representative in the case of any health, safety and environmental incident, including “Near-Miss”.
An initial written report is required by the end of the shift on which the incident occurs or the
SUBCONTRACTOR becomes aware of such incident. A detailed written incident report, including
root-cause analysis, will be provided to the WISON Representative within seven (7) calendar days
oftheincident’soccurrence.
WISONshallparticipateinaninvestigationcommitteeforallincidents,involving:
 FatalInjuries;
 LostTimeInjuries;

 FirstAidCase;

 Damage,inanyamount,toWISONorOWNERequipmentorproperty;

procedures compatible with WISON Site Emergency Response Plan, and shall provide all

The plan shall include emergency organization, emergency alarm systems, assembly points and
evacuation routes, head count process, and provisions for employee training before entering the

schedules shall be provided to the WISON within 30 days of construction commencement. These
drillsshallbeconductedonquarterlybasis.

11. HealthGoalsandObjectives
SUBCONTRACTOR shall provide information concerning its plans to support the Project Health
Implementationgoals andobjectives.Theprimarygoal oftheProjectHealthProgramis toprovidea
workplacethatisreasonablyfreeofrecognizedworkplacehealthhazards.
SUBCONTRACTOR shall explain how its program shall recognize, evaluate and control health
hazards. SUBCONTRACTORprogram shall include health hazard assessmentsand good industrial
hygiene practices. The SUBCONTRACTOR program shall specify how its program shall meet the
Projectobjectivesandgoalsandshalladdressthefollowing:
 Ensurethatallpotentialhealthhazardsareidentifiedandassessed.
 Maintainthehealthandwellbeingofworkplacepersonnelthroughmonitoringandsurveillance.
 Eliminateorcontrolworkplacehealthhazardstopreventoccupationalrelatedillnessesorinjuries.
 Characterizeworkplaceexposuretopotentialhealthhazards.
 Implement the Health findings in the Environmental Impact Assessment. These will be
summarizedintheEnvironmentalManagementPlan.
 Ensure compliance with recognized occupational safety and health standards and regulations to
includeprogramrequirementsandlocallaws,asapplicabletoeachsituation.
 Reduce costs associated with lost man-hours, medical treatment and surveillance and worker’s
compensation.
SUBCONTRACTORHealthprogramshallcontainthefollowingelements:
Page11of31

 HealthHazardEvaluation
Health Hazard Evaluations procedure shall be prepared for all applicable construction activities.
The assessment shall normally be performed in the form of a Job Hazard Analysis (JHA). The
Health Hazard Evaluation shall include potential chemical, biological, physical and ergonomic
hazards associated with the activities as well as an evaluation of any existing measures used to
controlthesehazards.
 HazardControl
When a chemical, physical or biological hazard is identified in a Health Hazard Evaluation, and
cannotbeeliminatedfromtheworkplace,theappropriatecontrol(s)thatarethenincorporated into
the work activity. Controls may include engineering, administrative or personal protective
equipment.Controlsshall bere-evaluatedwhenever aprocesschangeorother factormightaffect
thefrequencyorseverityofthehazard.
12. Ergonomics
ErgonomicActivitiesduringConstruction:
SUBCONTRACTOR shall include the requirements of WISON Ergonomics for Construction Phase
Procedure and Best Practice specification for application of ergonomics within construction
engineering,fabrication&installationwork.
13. HSEAuditsandAssessments
SUBCONTRACTOR shall establish a documented assessment process acceptable to WISON,
which measures compliance with the Project HSE Management System and SUBCONTRACTOR
own HSE requirement. SUBCONTRACTOR process shall also include scheduled audits of all its
lowertiersubcontractorsandtheirHSEManagementSystem,andfollow-upoffindingtoensurethat
the subcontractors implement and fully comply with the Project HSE Management System
requirements.
SUBCONTRACTOR shall use information derived from its assessment process in weekly HSE
coordination meeting to enhance supervisor safety awareness and improve overall
SUBCONTRACTORsafetyperformance.
WISON will perform periodic HSE assessments of the Project. SUBCONTRACTOR shall provide
WISON with timely, complete and open access to its safety process, files, records, etc., and shall
participateinthisassessmentas/ifrequested.
14. RiskManagement-IdentificationofHazardsandEnvironmentalAspects
14.1 GeneralIdentificationandEvaluation
SUBCONTRACTORshallprovidedetailsofitsriskmanagementprocess.
14.1.1 Identificationofhazardsandenvironmentalaspects
SUBCONTRACTOR shall be expected to incorporate the WISON Site Hazard Identification Plan.
ActionsfromthereviewshallrequiretheSUBCONTRACTORtoreviseitsHSEmanagementsystem.
Subsequent Identification of hazards and environmental aspects may be conducted as the
SUBCONTRACTORSworkprogress.
14.1.2 SafetyTaskAnalysisRiskReductionTalk(STARRT)
The SUBCONTRACTOR supervisor shall use the STARRT process, with participation from the
entire crew, to identify potential hazards associated with a particular task, just prior to its
commencement. The STARRT process shall be used at the beginning of every shift and prior to
Page12of31

starting any new task in the course of a shift. The process takes approximately fifteen (15) minutes
eachmorningorbeforebeginninganynewworktaskduringthecourseofashift.
SUBCONTRACTOR supervisors shall ensure that their employees understand the purpose of, and
participate in the JHA and STARRT processes and shall use them as primary planning and lessons
learnedtools.
14.2 WorkMethodStatements
SUBCONTRACTORshallprovidedetailsofitsworkmethodstatementsforeachsiteactivity.
14.3 JobHazardAnalysis(JHA)SafetyAnalysis(JSA)
JHA is used to identify, analyze, understand and mitigate potential hazards associated with
repetitiveorpotentiallyhazardousworkoperations.
SUBCONTRACTOR should use WISON Job Hazard Analysis system (JHA) and its employee pre-
task planning process known as Safety Task And Risk Reduction Talks (STARRT). If preferred,
SUBCONTRACTORmayuseitsownequivalentsystems,subjecttoWISONreviewandacceptance
shallfollow ADNOCHSEStandardsHSE-OS-ST03JobSafetyAnalysisStandard.
TheJHAJSAshallbeusedtodescribe workstepsandto identify, analyze,understand andmitigate
potential hazards associated with repetitive or potentially hazardous work operations engaged in
over a period of time. The JHA JSA format shall be provided by the WISON and completed by
SUBCONTRACTOR.
14.4 FormalRiskAssessment
The main goal of the Formal Risk Assessment is to identify all hazards and threats. It involves
assessing the risks associated with these hazards in worst-case scenarios using the ADNOC RAM.
The assessment also incorporates the principles of Hierarchy of Controls to determine the
necessary measures to reduce the risks to a level that is As Low as Reasonably Practicable
(ALARP).
As a result, all activities should be clearly detailed in the Method Statement and assessed using the
FormalRiskAssessment(FRA)approach.

ST05,RefertoAppendix-4.

Sustainable Development, as interpreted for the Project, refers to industrial development that
incorporates a set of interrelated principles and practices that mutually encourage economic
prosperity, environmental protection, and social & cultural equity. In general Sustainable
Developmentinitiativesshould:

 Maximizetheconservationofenergy;

 Supporteducationandtraininginitiatives.

15.3 SustainableDevelopmentPerformanceevaluation.
Sustainable Development Performance will be measured monthly based on the
SUBCONTRACTOR’S implementation of the SUBCONTRACTOR’S submitted and approved
sustainable development plan. This plan will contain specific, measurable, agreed (between ISON
and SUBCONTRACTOR) sustainable development objectives. This will include a schedule with
milestonestoobjectivelyevaluatetheSUBCONTRACTORSperformance.
Within sixty (60) calendar days after subcontract award or as otherwise agreed upon at the award
meeting, SUBCONTRACTOR shall submit its plan for Sustainable Development. After plan is
approved by the WISON, the SUBCONTRACTOR and WISON will establish the criteria to measure
the SUBCONTRACTOR performance each month with the agreed upon sustainable development
plan.
This documented performance evaluation will be reviewed and incorporated into the
SUBCONTRACTORmonthlyprogressreviewmeeting.

21. ToolsandPortablePowerTools
Portable Power Tools shall comply with ADNOC HSE Standards HSE-PSW-CP09 Electrical Safety,
with reference to Clause 7.5.10. work with portable (hand-held) electrical apparatus. A dead man
switchshallbeequippedforallportablepowertools.
Tools shall be equipped with proper safeguards and used only in applications for which they were
designed. Portable power tools shall be of the double insulated type or three-wire grounded type,
and should not be rated or used at a voltage exceeding 125 volts. All electrical outlets servicing
these power tools must be protected by Ground Fault Circuit Interrupters (GFCI) which meets
WISON requirements. All tools shall be free from any defect and maintained in a good operating
condition. WISON will reject tools found to be defective or of substandard tools. In this event,
SUBCONTRACTOR shall immediately replace the defective or substandard tools at
SUBCONTRACTOR sole expense. SUBCONTRACTOR shall follow all WISON procedures and
directives concerning the use, maintain and inspection of tools and portable power tools. In the

41. COSHH(HealthManagementProcedure)
41.1 PreparationofCOSHHAssessments
SUBCONTRACTOR shall provide COSHH Assessments to WISON for review and approval. The
COSHH Assessments shall be performed for all potentially hazardous substances that may
constituteanexposurehazardtositeworkersorotherstaff.Attachmentshallbeusedforcompletion
of the task. SUBCONTRACTOR on PROJECT site is required to provide these assessments and
any other relevant information for all hazardous materials they propose to use. Each assessment
shall consider the storage of the purchased materials, as well as all hazards from the proposed use
includinganysecondaryproducts,thatmayformduringtheactivityorwithintheprocess.
Copies of all assessments, the original product safety information (Material Safety Data Sheets)
providedbythesuppliers,andanyrelevantcorrespondenceshouldbeforwardedtotheWISONSite
HSEManagerforreviewandfiling.
Copies of MSDS and FirstAid instructions mustbe readily accessible to thosesupplying First Aid or
Medicalattention.
41.2 RiskControl
If the COSHH Assessment concludes that there are risks to health from storage or use of the
product, then action shall be taken to prevent or reduce the risk to an acceptable level. Due
considerationmustbegivento:
1) Substance Elimination: changing the process or activity so that the hazardous substance is not
requiredorgenerated.
2) Reductionofquantitiesstoredorused.
3) Substitution with a safer alternative or a safer form of the same substance (for example, pellets in
placeofpowder,ormoredilutesolutions).
4) If prevention of the hazard through one of the above means is not reasonably practical then a
controlstrategyshallbeselectedfromthefollowinglist(singlyorincombination):
 Totalenclosureofthesubstance(suchasaglovebox);
 Partialenclosureandlocalexhaustventilation;
 Generalventilation(Includingnaturalventilationifeffective);
 Specialprocedures.
 Reliance on personal protective equipment (PPE) such as respiratory protection should only be
used as a last resort if all the above strategies result in an unacceptable level of risk. However,
PPE shall be worn, as appropriate, commensurate with the requirements of the activity being
conducted.

variety of complex inspection functions with minimal supervision.

| 3.6 | Inspection,      | Measuring      | and Test   | Equipment |               |                |
3.6.1 The used Device or Equipment shall be calibrated or verified, or both, at
specified intervals, or prior to use, against measurement standards
traceable to international or national measurement standards; where no
such standards exist, thebasis used forcalibration orverification shall be
recorded.
3.6.2 Every Device or Equipment which is used for the inspection must be
|     | calibrated | by the third | party. |     |     |     |
| --- | ---------- | ------------ | ------ | --- | --- | --- |
3.6.3 Calibration Log shall be prepared and maintained by the Device
Controller. The device situation shall be clearly identified in the log
including: Identification No. , Size-Range, Model No., Calibration
|     | Frequency | Date, Calibrated, |     | Calibration | expired Date | etc. |
| --- | --------- | ----------------- | --- | ----------- | ------------ | ---- |
3.6.4 Each item requiring calibration shall be individually identified by serial
number.Acalibration tag or sticker, which includes the serial number and
the calibration due date, shall be affixed where physically possible.
3.6.5 Device or Equipment shall be calibrated to or checked basis on
manufacture’s specification and international customary practices.
3.6.6 The reference standards used for calibration shall have accuracy levels
and ranges that are greater than those of the Device or Equipment being
calibrated.
3.6.7 Device or Equipment calibration report shall be issued by third party in
|     | accordance   | with the  | standard | requirement. |     |     |

e) A timeline for the implementation of safety and quality control measures.
5.2 Baseline Schedule
5.2.1 The Subcontractor shall further develop its Tender Schedule into a
comprehensive, resource-loaded Level 4 Baseline Schedule. This shall be
submitted for the Contractor's review and approval within fourteen (14) calendar
days of the Contract Effective Date.
5.2.2 In addition to fulfilling the Tender Schedule requirements, the Baseline Schedule
must incorporate:
• All critical Project Milestones.
• A detailed plan for preliminary works, site preparation, and mobilization.
• Procurement and delivery schedules for all key materials and equipment (if
applicable).
• A detailed work front handover and interface plan.
5.2.3 An updated Basis of Schedule narrative shall be submitted concurrently with the
Baseline Schedule.
5.2.4 The Contractor shall review the submitted Baseline Schedule within fourteen (14)
calendar days of receipt. Once approved, this Baseline Schedule shall serve as
the official benchmark for all progress control and performance evaluation.
5.2.5 The Contractor's review, comment, approval, or lack of response to any schedule
submission shall not relieve the Subcontractor of any of its obligations to comply
with the Contract requirements, nor shall it be construed as an acceptance of:
 Non-compliance with the KEY MILESTONE DATES.
 Any adverse modifications to the logic or durations within the Baseline
Schedule or its updates.
 Inaccurate logic, constraints, or unreasonable activity durations.
 Any schedule that imposes undue hindrance on the works of other
subcontractors.
 A waiver of the Contractor's rights to demand timely performance or to
exercise its remedies in the event of default by the Subcontractor.
5.2.6 The Baseline Schedule shall be updated weekly to serve as the Progress
Schedule, showing a comparison between the actual/forecast completion of all
activities and the baseline dates. Furthermore, the Subcontractor shall, with the
Contractor's approval, formally update the Baseline Schedule as a Recovery Plan
(with no change to the Completion Date) or a Revised Plan (only when the

plan at any time. The Recovery Plan must include, as the minimum:

b) A description of the specific corrective measures to be implemented.

| d) A detailed | plan     | for any | required      | increase | in resources.     |     |
